  4. Let response be responseObject’s [response](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-response-response). 
  5. If response’s [body](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-response-body) is non-null and is [readable](https://streams.spec.whatwg.org/#readablestream-readable), then [error](https://streams.spec.whatwg.org/#readablestream-error) response’s [body](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-response-body) with error. 


A `FetchLaterResult[](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#fetchlaterresult)` has an associated activated getter steps, which is an algorithm returning a boolean. 
The `activated` getter steps are to return the result of running [this](https://webidl.spec.whatwg.org/#this)’s [activated getter steps](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#fetchlaterresult-activated-getter-steps). 
The `fetchLater(input, init)` method steps are: 
  1. Let requestObject be the result of invoking the initial value of `Request[](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#request)` as constructor with input and init as arguments. 
  2. If requestObject’s [signal](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#request-signal) is [aborted](https://dom.spec.whatwg.org/#abortsignal-aborted), then throw [signal](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#request-signal)’s [abort reason](https://dom.spec.whatwg.org/#abortsignal-abort-reason). 
  3. Let request be requestObject’s [request](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-request-request). 
  4. Let activateAfter be null. 
  5. If init is given and init["`activateAfter[](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#dom-deferredrequestinit-activateafter)`"] [exists](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#map-exists), then set activateAfter to init["`activateAfter[](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#dom-deferredrequestinit-activateafter)`"]. 
  6. If activateAfter is less than 0, then throw a `RangeError[](https://webidl.spec.whatwg.org/#exceptiondef-rangeerror)`. 
  7. If [this](https://webidl.spec.whatwg.org/#this)’s [relevant global object](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#concept-relevant-global)’s [associated document](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/nav-history-apis.html#concept-document-window) is not [fully active](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/document-sequences.html#fully-active), then throw a `TypeError[](https://webidl.spec.whatwg.org/#exceptiondef-typeerror)`. 
  8. If request’s [URL](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-request-url)’s [scheme](https://url.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-url-scheme) is not an [HTTP(S) scheme](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#http-scheme), then throw a `TypeError[](https://webidl.spec.whatwg.org/#exceptiondef-typeerror)`. 
  9. If request’s [URL](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-request-url) is not a [potentially trustworthy URL](https://w3c.github.io/webappsec-secure-contexts/#potentially-trustworthy-url), then throw a `TypeError[](https://webidl.spec.whatwg.org/#exceptiondef-typeerror)`. 
  10. If request’s [body](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-request-body) is not null, and request’s [body](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-request-body) [length](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-body-total-bytes) is null, then throw a `TypeError[](https://webidl.spec.whatwg.org/#exceptiondef-typeerror)`. 
Requests whose [body](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-request-body) is a `ReadableStream[](https://streams.spec.whatwg.org/#readablestream)` object cannot be deferred. 
  11. Let quota be the [available deferred-fetch quota](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#available-deferred-fetch-quota) given request’s [client](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-request-client) and request’s [URL](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-request-url)’s [origin](https://url.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-url-origin). 
  12. Let requested be request’s [total request length](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#total-request-length). 
  13. If quota is less than requested, then [throw](https://webidl.spec.whatwg.org/#dfn-throw) a `QuotaExceededError[](https://webidl.spec.whatwg.org/#quotaexceedederror)` whose [quota](https://webidl.spec.whatwg.org/#quotaexceedederror-quota) is quota and [requested](https://webidl.spec.whatwg.org/#quotaexceedederror-requested) is requested. 
  14. Let activated be false. 
  15. Let deferredRecord be the result of calling [queue a deferred fetch](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#queue-a-deferred-fetch) given request, activateAfter, and the following step: set activated to true. 
  16. [Add the following abort steps](https://dom.spec.whatwg.org/#abortsignal-add) to requestObject’s [signal](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#request-signal): Set deferredRecord’s [invoke state](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#deferred-fetch-record-invoke-state) to "`aborted`". 
  17. Return a new `FetchLaterResult[](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#fetchlaterresult)` whose [activated getter steps](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#fetchlaterresult-activated-getter-steps) are to return activated. 


The following call would queue a request to be fetched when the document is terminated: 

```
fetchLater("https://report.example.com", {
  method: "POST",
  body: JSON.stringify(myReport),
  headers: { "Content-Type": "application/json" }
})

```

The following call would also queue this request after 5 seconds, and the returned value would allow callers to observe if it was indeed activated. Note that the request is guaranteed to be invoked, even in cases where the user agent throttles timers. 

```
const result = fetchLater("https://report.example.com", {
  method: "POST",
  body: JSON.stringify(myReport),
  headers: { "Content-Type": "application/json" },
  activateAfter: 5000
});

function check_if_fetched() {
  return result.activated;
}

```

The `FetchLaterResult[](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#fetchlaterresult)` object can be used together with an `AbortSignal[](https://dom.spec.whatwg.org/#abortsignal)`. For example: 

```
let accumulated_events = [];
let previous_result = null;
const abort_signal = new AbortSignal();
function accumulate_event(event) {
  if (previous_result) {
    if (previous_result.activated) {
      // The request is already activated, we can start from scratch.
      accumulated_events = [];
    } else {
      // Abort this request, and start a new one with all the events.
      signal.abort();
    }
  }

  accumulated_events.push(event);
  result = fetchLater("https://report.example.com", {
    method: "POST",
    body: JSON.stringify(accumulated_events),
    headers: { "Content-Type": "application/json" },
    activateAfter: 5000,
    abort_signal
  });
}

```

Any of the following calls to [`fetchLater()`](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#dom-window-fetchlater) would throw: 

```
// Only [potentially trustworthy URL](https://w3c.github.io/webappsec-secure-contexts/#potentially-trustworthy-url)s are supported.
fetchLater("http://untrusted.example.com");

// The length of the deferred request has to be known when.
fetchLater("https://origin.example.com", {body: someDynamicStream});

// Deferred fetching only works on active windows.
const detachedWindow = iframe.contentWindow;
iframe.remove();
detachedWindow.fetchLater("https://origin.example.com");

```

See [deferred fetch quota examples](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#deferred-fetch-quota-examples) for examples portraying how the deferred-fetch quota works. 
###  5.7. Garbage collection
The user agent may [terminate](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#fetch-controller-terminate) an ongoing fetch if that termination is not observable through script. 
"Observable through script" means observable through [`fetch()`](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#dom-global-fetch)’s arguments and return value. Other ways, such as communicating with the server through a side-channel are not included. 
The server being able to observe garbage collection has precedent, e.g., with `WebSocket[](https://websockets.spec.whatwg.org/#websocket)` and `XMLHttpRequest[](https://xhr.spec.whatwg.org/#xmlhttprequest)` objects. 
The user agent can terminate the fetch because the termination cannot be observed. 

```
fetch("https://www.example.com/")

```

The user agent cannot terminate the fetch because the termination can be observed through the promise. 

```
window.promise = fetch("https://www.example.com/")

```

The user agent can terminate the fetch because the associated body is not observable. 

```
window.promise = fetch("https://www.example.com/").then(res => res.headers)

```

The user agent can terminate the fetch because the termination cannot be observed. 

```
fetch("https://www.example.com/").then(res => res.body.getReader().closed)

```

The user agent cannot terminate the fetch because one can observe the termination by registering a handler for the promise object. 

```
window.promise = fetch("https://www.example.com/")
  .then(res => res.body.getReader().closed)

```

The user agent cannot terminate the fetch as termination would be observable via the registered handler. 

```
fetch("https://www.example.com/")
  .then(res => {
    res.body.getReader().closed.then(() => console.log("stream closed!"))
  })

```

(The above examples of non-observability assume that built-in properties and functions, such as `body.getReader()[](https://streams.spec.whatwg.org/#rs-get-reader)`, have not been overwritten.) 
##  6. `data:` URLs
For an informative description of `data:` URLs, see RFC 2397. This section replaces that RFC’s normative processing requirements to be compatible with deployed content. [[RFC2397]](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#biblio-rfc2397 "The "data" URL scheme")
A `data:` URL struct is a [struct](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct) that consists of a MIME type (a [MIME type](https://mimesniff.spec.whatwg.org/#mime-type)) and a body (a [byte sequence](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#byte-sequence)). 
The `data:` URL processor takes a [URL](https://url.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-url) dataURL and then runs these steps: 
  1. [Assert](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#assert): dataURL’s [scheme](https://url.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-url-scheme) is "`data`". 
  2. Let input be the result of running the [URL serializer](https://url.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-url-serializer) on dataURL with [_exclude fragment_](https://url.spec.whatwg.org/#url-serializer-exclude-fragment) set to true. 
  3. Remove the leading "`data:`" from input. 
  4. Let position point at the start of input. 
  5. Let mimeType be the result of [collecting a sequence of code points](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#collect-a-sequence-of-code-points) that are not equal to U+002C (,), given position. 
  6. [Strip leading and trailing ASCII whitespace](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#strip-leading-and-trailing-ascii-whitespace) from mimeType. 
This will only remove U+0020 SPACE [code points](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#code-point), if any. 
  7. If position is past the end of input, then return failure. 
  8. Advance position by 1. 
  9. Let encodedBody be the remainder of input. 
  10. Let body be the [percent-decoding](https://url.spec.whatwg.org/#string-percent-decode) of encodedBody. 
  11. If mimeType ends with U+003B (;), followed by zero or more U+0020 SPACE, followed by an [ASCII case-insensitive](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#ascii-case-insensitive) match for "`base64`", then: 
    1. Let stringBody be the [isomorphic decode](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#isomorphic-decode) of body. 
    2. Set body to the [forgiving-base64 decode](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#forgiving-base64-decode) of stringBody. 
    3. If body is failure, then return failure. 
    4. Remove the last 6 [code points](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#code-point) from mimeType. 
    5. Remove trailing U+0020 SPACE [code points](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#code-point) from mimeType, if any. 
    6. Remove the last U+003B (;) from mimeType. 
  12. If mimeType [starts with](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#string-starts-with) "`;`", then prepend "`text/plain`" to mimeType. 
  13. Let mimeTypeRecord be the result of [parsing](https://mimesniff.spec.whatwg.org/#parse-a-mime-type) mimeType. 
  14. If mimeTypeRecord is failure, then set mimeTypeRecord to `text/plain;charset=US-ASCII`. 
  15. Return a new [`data:` URL struct](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#data-url-struct) whose [MIME type](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#data-url-struct-mime-type) is mimeTypeRecord and [body](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#data-url-struct-body) is body. 


## Background reading
_This section and its subsections are informative only._
### HTTP header layer division
For the purposes of fetching, there is an API layer (HTML’s `img`, CSS’s `background-image`), early fetch layer, service worker layer, and network & cache layer. ``Accept`` and ``Accept-Language`` are set in the early fetch layer (typically by the user agent). Most other headers controlled by the user agent, such as ``Accept-Encoding``, ``Host``, and ``Referer``, are set in the network & cache layer. Developers can set headers either at the API layer or in the service worker layer (typically through a `Request[](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#request)` object). Developers have almost no control over [forbidden request-headers](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#forbidden-request-header), but can control ``Accept`` and have the means to constrain and omit ``Referer`` for instance. 
### Atomic HTTP redirect handling
Redirects (a [response](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-response) whose [status](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-response-status) or [internal response](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-internal-response)’s (if any) [status](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-response-status) is a [redirect status](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#redirect-status)) are not exposed to APIs. Exposing redirects might leak information not otherwise available through a cross-site scripting attack. 
A fetch to `https://example.org/auth` that includes a `Cookie` marked `HttpOnly` could result in a redirect to `https://other-origin.invalid/4af955781ea1c84a3b11`. This new URL contains a secret. If we expose redirects that secret would be available through a cross-site scripting attack. 
### Basic safe CORS protocol setup
For resources where data is protected through IP authentication or a firewall (unfortunately relatively common still), using the [CORS protocol](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#cors-protocol) is **unsafe**. (This is the reason why the [CORS protocol](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#cors-protocol) had to be invented.) 
However, otherwise using the following [header](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-header) is **safe** : 

```
Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *

```

Even if a resource exposes additional information based on cookie or HTTP authentication, using the above [header](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-header) will not reveal it. It will share the resource with APIs such as `XMLHttpRequest[](https://xhr.spec.whatwg.org/#xmlhttprequest)`, much like it is already shared with `curl` and `wget`. 
Thus in other words, if a resource cannot be accessed from a random device connected to the web using `curl` and `wget` the aforementioned [header](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-header) is not to be included. If it can be accessed however, it is perfectly fine to do so. 
### CORS protocol and HTTP caches
If [CORS protocol](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#cors-protocol) requirements are more complicated than setting `[`Access-Control-Allow-Origin`](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#http-access-control-allow-origin)` to `*` or a static [origin](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/browsers.html#concept-origin), ``Vary`` is to be used. [[HTML]](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#biblio-html "HTML Standard") [[HTTP]](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#biblio-http "HTTP Semantics") [[HTTP-CACHING]](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#biblio-http-caching "HTTP Caching")

```
Vary: Origin

```

In particular, consider what happens if ``Vary`` is _not_ used and a server is configured to send `[`Access-Control-Allow-Origin`](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#http-access-control-allow-origin)` for a certain resource only in response to a [CORS request](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#cors-request). When a user agent receives a response to a non-[CORS request](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#cors-request) for that resource (for example, as the result of a [navigation request](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#navigation-request)), the response will lack `[`Access-Control-Allow-Origin`](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#http-access-control-allow-origin)` and the user agent will cache that response. Then, if the user agent subsequently encounters a [CORS request](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#cors-request) for the resource, it will use that cached response from the previous non-[CORS request](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#cors-request), without `[`Access-Control-Allow-Origin`](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#http-access-control-allow-origin)`. 
But if ``Vary: Origin`` is used in the same scenario described above, it will cause the user agent to [fetch](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-fetch) a response that includes `[`Access-Control-Allow-Origin`](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#http-access-control-allow-origin)`, rather than using the cached response from the previous non-[CORS request](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#cors-request) that lacks `[`Access-Control-Allow-Origin`](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#http-access-control-allow-origin)`. 
However, if `[`Access-Control-Allow-Origin`](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#http-access-control-allow-origin)` is set to `*` or a static [origin](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/browsers.html#concept-origin) for a particular resource, then configure the server to always send `[`Access-Control-Allow-Origin`](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#http-access-control-allow-origin)` in responses for the resource — for non-[CORS requests](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#cors-request) as well as [CORS requests](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#cors-request) — and do not use ``Vary``. 
### WebSockets
As part of establishing a connection, the `WebSocket[](https://websockets.spec.whatwg.org/#websocket)` object initiates a special kind of [fetch](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-fetch) (using a [request](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-request) whose [mode](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-request-mode) is "`websocket`") which allows it to share in many fetch policy decisions, such HTTP Strict Transport Security (HSTS). Ultimately this results in fetch calling into WebSockets to obtain a dedicated connection. [[WEBSOCKETS]](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#biblio-websockets "WebSockets Standard") [[HSTS]](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#biblio-hsts "HTTP Strict Transport Security \(HSTS\)")
Fetch used to define [obtain a WebSocket connection](https://websockets.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-websocket-connection-obtain) and [establish a WebSocket connection](https://websockets.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-websocket-establish) directly, but both are now defined in WebSockets. [[WEBSOCKETS]](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#biblio-websockets "WebSockets Standard")
## Using fetch in other standards
In its essence [fetching](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-fetch) is an exchange of a [request](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-request) for a [response](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-response). In reality it is rather complex mechanism for standards to adopt and use correctly. This section aims to give some advice. 
Always ask domain experts for review. 
This is a work in progress. 
### Setting up a request
The first step in [fetching](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-fetch) is to create a [request](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-request), and populate its [items](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct-item). 
Start by setting the [request](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-request)’s [URL](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-request-url) and [method](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-request-method), as defined by HTTP. If your ``POST`` or ``PUT`` [request](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-request) needs a body, you set [request](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-request)’s [body](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-request-body) to a [byte sequence](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#byte-sequence), or to a new [body](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-body) whose [stream](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-body-stream) is a `ReadableStream[](https://streams.spec.whatwg.org/#readablestream)` you created. [[HTTP]](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#biblio-http "HTTP Semantics")
Choose your [request](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-request)’s [destination](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-request-destination) using the guidance in the [destination table](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#destination-table). [Destinations](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-request-destination) affect Content Security Policy and have other implications such as the ``Sec-Fetch-Dest[](https://w3c.github.io/webappsec-fetch-metadata/#http-headerdef-sec-fetch-dest)`` header, so they are much more than informative metadata. If a new feature requires a [destination](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-request-destination) that’s not in the [destination table](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#destination-table), please [file an issue](https://github.com/whatwg/fetch/issues/new?title=What%20destination%20should%20my%20feature%20use) to discuss. [[CSP]](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#biblio-csp "Content Security Policy Level 3")
Set your [request](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-request)’s [client](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-request-client) to the [environment settings object](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#environment-settings-object) you’re operating in. Web-exposed APIs are generally defined with Web IDL, for which every object that implements an [interface](https://webidl.spec.whatwg.org/#dfn-interface) has a [relevant settings object](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#relevant-settings-object) you can use. For example, a [request](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-request) associated with an [element](https://dom.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-element) would set the [request](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-request)’s [client](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-request-client) to the element’s [node document](https://dom.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-node-document)’s [relevant settings object](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#relevant-settings-object). All features that are directly web-exposed by JavaScript, HTML, CSS, or other `Document[](https://dom.spec.whatwg.org/#document)` subresources should have a [client](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-request-client). 
If your [fetching](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-fetch) is not directly web-exposed, e.g., it is sent in the background without relying on a current `Window[](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/nav-history-apis.html#window)` or `Worker[](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/workers.html#worker)`, leave [request](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-request)’s [client](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-request-client) as null and set the [request](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-request)’s [origin](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-request-origin), [policy container](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-request-policy-container), [service-workers mode](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#request-service-workers-mode), and [referrer](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-request-referrer) to appropriate values instead, e.g., by copying them from the [environment settings object](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#environment-settings-object) ahead of time. In these more advanced cases, make sure the details of how your fetch handles Content Security Policy and [referrer policy](https://w3c.github.io/webappsec-referrer-policy/#referrer-policy) are fleshed out. Also make sure you handle concurrency, as callbacks (see [Invoking fetch and processing responses](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#fetch-elsewhere-fetch)) would be posted on a [parallel queue](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/infrastructure.html#parallel-queue). [[REFERRER]](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#biblio-referrer "Referrer Policy") [[CSP]](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#biblio-csp "Content Security Policy Level 3")
Think through the way you intend to handle cross-origin resources. Some features may only work in the [same origin](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/browsers.html#same-origin), in which case set your [request](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-request)’s [mode](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-request-mode) to "`same-origin`". Otherwise, new web-exposed features should almost always set their [mode](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-request-mode) to "`cors`". If your feature is not web-exposed, or you think there is another reason for it to fetch cross-origin resources without CORS, please [file an issue](https://github.com/whatwg/fetch/issues/new?title=Does%20my%20request%20require%20CORS) to discuss. 
For cross-origin requests, also determines if [credentials](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#credentials) are to be included with the requests, in which case set your [request](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-request)’s [credentials mode](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-request-credentials-mode) to "`include`". 
Figure out if your fetch needs to be reported to Resource Timing, and with which [initiator type](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#request-initiator-type). By passing an [initiator type](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#request-initiator-type) to the [request](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-request), reporting to Resource Timing will be done automatically once the fetch is done and the [response](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-response) is fully downloaded. [[RESOURCE-TIMING]](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#biblio-resource-timing "Resource Timing")
If your request requires additional HTTP headers, set its [header list](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-request-header-list) to a [header list](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-header-list) that contains those headers, e.g., « (``My-Header-Name``, ``My-Header-Value``) ». Sending custom headers may have implications, such as requiring a [CORS-preflight fetch](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#cors-preflight-fetch-0), so handle with care. 
If you want to override the default caching mechanism, e.g., disable caching for this [request](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-request), set the request’s [cache mode](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-request-cache-mode) to a value other than "`default`". 
Determine whether you want your request to support redirects. If you don’t, set its [redirect mode](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-request-redirect-mode) to "`error`". 
Browse through the rest of the parameters for [request](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-request) to see if something else is relevant to you. The rest of the parameters are used less frequently, often for special purposes, and they are documented in detail in the [§ 2.2.5 Requests](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#requests) section of this standard. 
### Invoking fetch and processing responses
Aside from a [request](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-request) the [fetch](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-fetch) operation takes several optional arguments. For those arguments that take an algorithm: the algorithm will be called from a task (or in a [parallel queue](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/infrastructure.html#parallel-queue) if [_useParallelQueue_](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#fetch-useparallelqueue) is true). 
Once the [request](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-request) is set up, to determine which algorithms to pass to [fetch](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-fetch), determine how you would like to process the [response](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-response), and in particular at what stage you would like to receive a callback:  

Upon completion 
    
This is how most callers handle a [response](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-response), for example [scripts](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#fetch-a-classic-script) and [style resources](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-values-4/#fetch-a-style-resource). The [response](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-response)’s [body](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-response-body) is read in its entirety into a [byte sequence](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#byte-sequence), and then processed by the caller. 
To process a [response](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-response) upon completion, pass an algorithm as the [_processResponseConsumeBody_](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#process-response-end-of-body) argument of [fetch](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-fetch). The given algorithm is passed a [response](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-response) and an argument representing the fully read [body](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-response-body) (of the [response](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-response)’s [internal response](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-internal-response)). The second argument’s values have the following meaning:  

null 
    The [response](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-response)’s [body](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-response-body) is null, due to the response being a [network error](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-network-error) or having a [null body status](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#null-body-status).  

failure 
    Attempting to [fully read](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#body-fully-read) the contents of the [response](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-response)’s [body](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-response-body) failed, e.g., due to an I/O error.  

a [byte sequence](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#byte-sequence) 
    
[Fully reading](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#body-fully-read) the contents of the [response](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-response)’s [internal response](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-internal-response)’s [body](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-response-body) succeeded. 
A [byte sequence](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#byte-sequence) containing the full contents will be passed also for a [request](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-request) whose [mode](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-request-mode) is "`no-cors`". Callers have to be careful when handling such content, as it should not be accessible to the requesting [origin](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/browsers.html#concept-origin). For example, the caller may use contents of a "`no-cors`" [response](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-response) to display image contents directly to the user, but those image contents should not be directly exposed to scripts in the embedding document. 
  1. Let request be a [request](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-request) whose [URL](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-request-url) is `https://stuff.example.com/` and [client](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-request-client) is [this](https://webidl.spec.whatwg.org/#this)’s [relevant settings object](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#relevant-settings-object). 
  2. [Fetch](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-fetch) request, with [_processResponseConsumeBody_](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#process-response-end-of-body) set to the following steps given a [response](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-response) response and null, failure, or a [byte sequence](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#byte-sequence) contents: 
    1. If contents is null or failure, then present an error to the user. 
    2. Otherwise, parse contents considering the metadata from response, and perform your own operations on it. 



Headers first, then chunk-by-chunk 
    
In some cases, for example when playing video or progressively loading images, callers might want to stream the response, and process it one chunk at a time. The [response](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-response) is handed over to the fetch caller once the headers are processed, and the caller continues from there. 
To process a [response](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-response) chunk-by-chunk, pass an algorithm to the [_processResponse_](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#process-response) argument of [fetch](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-fetch). The given algorithm is passed a [response](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-response) when the response’s headers have been received and is responsible for reading the [response](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-response)’s [body](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-response-body)’s [stream](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-body-stream) in order to download the rest of the response. For convenience, you may also pass an algorithm to the [_processResponseEndOfBody_](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#fetch-processresponseendofbody) argument, which is called once you have finished fully reading the response and its [body](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-response-body). Note that unlike [_processResponseConsumeBody_](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#process-response-end-of-body), passing the [_processResponse_](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#process-response) or [_processResponseEndOfBody_](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#fetch-processresponseendofbody) arguments does not guarantee that the response will be fully read, and callers are responsible to read it themselves. 
The [_processResponse_](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#process-response) argument is also useful for handling the [response](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-response)’s [header list](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-response-header-list) and [status](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-response-status) without handling the [body](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-response-body) at all. This is used, for example, when handling responses that do not have an [ok status](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#ok-status). 
  1. Let request be a [request](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-request) whose [URL](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-request-url) is `https://stream.example.com/` and [client](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-request-client) is [this](https://webidl.spec.whatwg.org/#this)’s [relevant settings object](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#relevant-settings-object). 
  2. [Fetch](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-fetch) request, with [_processResponse_](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#process-response) set to the following steps given a [response](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-response) response: 
    1. If response is a [network error](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-network-error), then present an error to the user. 
    2. Otherwise, if response’s [status](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-response-status) is not an [ok status](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#ok-status), present some fallback value to the user. 
    3. Otherwise, [get a reader](https://streams.spec.whatwg.org/#readablestream-get-a-reader) for [response](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-response)’s [body](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-response-body)’s [stream](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-body-stream), and process in an appropriate way for the MIME type identified by [extracting a MIME type](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-header-extract-mime-type) from response’s [headers list](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-response-header-list). 



Ignore the response 
    
In some cases, there is no need for a [response](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-response) at all, e.g., in the case of `navigator.sendBeacon()[](https://www.w3.org/TR/beacon/#dom-navigator-sendbeacon)`. Processing a response and passing callbacks to [fetch](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-fetch) is optional, so omitting the callback would [fetch](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-fetch) without expecting a response. In such cases, the [response](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-response)’s [body](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-response-body)’s [stream](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-body-stream) will be discarded, and the caller does not have to worry about downloading the contents unnecessarily. 
[Fetch](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-fetch) a [request](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-request) whose [URL](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-request-url) is `https://fire-and-forget.example.com/`, [method](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-request-method) is ``POST``, and [client](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-request-client) is [this](https://webidl.spec.whatwg.org/#this)’s [relevant settings object](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#relevant-settings-object). 
Apart from the callbacks to handle responses, [fetch](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-fetch) accepts additional callbacks for advanced cases. [_processEarlyHintsResponse_](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#fetch-processearlyhintsresponse) is intended specifically for [responses](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-response) whose [status](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-response-status) is 103, and is currently handled only by navigations. [_processRequestBodyChunkLength_](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#process-request-body) and [_processRequestEndOfBody_](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#process-request-end-of-body) notify the caller of request body uploading progress. 
Note that the [fetch](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-fetch) operation starts in the same thread from which it was called, and then breaks off to run its internal operations [in parallel](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/infrastructure.html#in-parallel). The aforementioned callbacks are posted to a given [event loop](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#event-loop) which is, by default, the [client](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-request-client)’s [global object](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#concept-settings-object-global). To process responses [in parallel](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/infrastructure.html#in-parallel) and handle interactions with the main thread by yourself, [fetch](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-fetch) with [_useParallelQueue_](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#fetch-useparallelqueue) set to true. 
### Manipulating an ongoing fetch
To manipulate a [fetch](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-fetch) operation that has already started, use the [fetch controller](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#fetch-controller) returned by calling [fetch](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-fetch). For example, you may [abort](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#fetch-controller-abort) the [fetch controller](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#fetch-controller) due the user or page logic, or [terminate](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#fetch-controller-terminate) it due to browser-internal circumstances. 
In addition to terminating and aborting, callers may [report timing](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#finalize-and-report-timing) if this was not done automatically by passing the [initiator type](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#request-initiator-type), or [extract full timing info](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#extract-full-timing-info) and handle it on the caller side (this is done only by navigations). The [fetch controller](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#fetch-controller) is also used to [process the next manual redirect](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#fetch-controller-process-the-next-manual-redirect) for [requests](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-request) with [redirect mode](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-request-redirect-mode) set to "`manual`".

  [constructor](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#dom-headers)(optional [HeadersInit](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#typedefdef-headersinit) [init](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#dom-headers-headers-init-init));

  [undefined](https://webidl.spec.whatwg.org/#idl-undefined) [append](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#dom-headers-append)([ByteString](https://webidl.spec.whatwg.org/#idl-ByteString) [name](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#dom-headers-append-name-value-name), [ByteString](https://webidl.spec.whatwg.org/#idl-ByteString) [value](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#dom-headers-append-name-value-value));
  [undefined](https://webidl.spec.whatwg.org/#idl-undefined) [delete](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#dom-headers-delete)([ByteString](https://webidl.spec.whatwg.org/#idl-ByteString) [name](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#dom-headers-delete-name-name));
  [ByteString](https://webidl.spec.whatwg.org/#idl-ByteString)? [get](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#dom-headers-get)([ByteString](https://webidl.spec.whatwg.org/#idl-ByteString) [name](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#dom-headers-get-name-name));
  [sequence](https://webidl.spec.whatwg.org/#idl-sequence)<[ByteString](https://webidl.spec.whatwg.org/#idl-ByteString)> [getSetCookie](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#dom-headers-getsetcookie)();
  [boolean](https://webidl.spec.whatwg.org/#idl-boolean) [has](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#dom-headers-has)([ByteString](https://webidl.spec.whatwg.org/#idl-ByteString) [name](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#dom-headers-has-name-name));
  [undefined](https://webidl.spec.whatwg.org/#idl-undefined) [set](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#dom-headers-set)([ByteString](https://webidl.spec.whatwg.org/#idl-ByteString) [name](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#dom-headers-set-name-value-name), [ByteString](https://webidl.spec.whatwg.org/#idl-ByteString) [value](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#dom-headers-set-name-value-value));
  [iterable](https://webidl.spec.whatwg.org/#dfn-iterable)<[ByteString](https://webidl.spec.whatwg.org/#idl-ByteString), [ByteString](https://webidl.spec.whatwg.org/#idl-ByteString)>;
};
